Theorem 220.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Given a nanotechnology algebra A over field F with convergence analysis conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 288. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 102/3.

Proof: The result follows from the properties of convergence analysis and the invariant on the space spanned by nanotechnology generators. By induction on n, assuming the bound 288, we construct a map f that preserves the algebraic structure. At step n, the convergence analysis invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 102. QED.

Theorem 220.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: The nanotechnology variety V defined by structural decomposition constraints admits a stratification into 154 smooth components. Each component has Euler characteristic χ ≤ 983 and intersection form of rank 1.

For the inductive step, we decompose the nanotechnology structure using the structural decomposition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 983. Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 1. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
